ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u22	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you very much, sir, for this question. The case is extremely sensitive because it concerns children. We always just have to follow the good of the children. These matters, and there are unfortunately more and more of them, result from the fact that such marriages, mixed marriages, are many. Indeed, I would like to say to you and your fellow Members that the Ministry of Justice has prepared a reform of the procedures under the Hague Convention in this respect, as a problem has been noted. The problem is, in principle, that the Polish citizen is often in a lost position, colliding in legal proceedings with nationals of other countries. These are extremely difficult procedures - I am talking about procedures under the Hague Convention - these are procedures in which civil elements, criminal elements, elements of international law appear. You have to have a lot of knowledge. You're right, but you still have to be able to defend that right from the legal side. Indeed, our citizens often lose when there are such disputes. Some take that they're right, because they're just right, and all of this has to be proved in court. The government has noticed this problem. When I took supervision of the department that dealt with these cases, I addressed the first steps to the national prosecutor. I asked him that prosecutors, as public-interest spokespersons, spokespersons of the rule of law, you might say, appear on every such case, in every case. This is an extremely important issue, because the spokesperson, who is the prosecutor in this case, can objectively, from the point of view of the public interest, assess the case in question. In this respect, guidance has been issued to prosecutors. This is a major change. In addition, I would like to tell you that prosecutors are involved in any such case, they are taking part in any such case which is taking place anywhere in Poland. But it's just one of the elements. Another important element is that we have noticed that such fragmentation in the courts that deal with these matters under the Hague Convention is very dangerous. It is three hundred and several dozen courts in Poland, regional courts. We have decided to prepare a proposal for a change in the fact that these three hundred and dozen courts will be replaced by district courts. It's not regional, it's district, which means we're raising it to a level higher. These 340 courts will be replaced by 11 courts. Only 11 courts in Poland will deal with these matters. As regards appeals: not 45 district courts, only one appeal court. There will be a department at the Appeal Court in Warsaw. It's kind of a specialty. In other words, not 10,000. The judges will deal with these cases, only on a national scale. It's important. Case two is a matter of solicitor's coercion. So I started my speech: these are very difficult things. Citizens often feel that they can defend themselves, and that is not the case, and that is why we will be proposing regulations, regulations on solicitor's coercion. The citizen will have to be represented by a lawyer. If he can't afford a defense attorney, if he can't afford one, then of course he'll get that public defender. This is another major change, something that hasn't been there yet. Case three is a cassation complaint, an emergency appeal, which will be in the hands of the Minister of Justice, the Attorney General, in exceptional cases. Today, the Minister of Justice - the Attorney General, even if he has the knowledge that he should intervene, actually has a very limited catalogue of possibilities. This is due to the fact that there is not this extraordinary measure, this tool, an instrument in the form of a cassation complaint. The fourth important point, namely the following fact. Because children in Poland are released after the first instance. Even if the ruling is unlawful, the child may be issued abroad in accordance with Polish regulations. This means that if a decision is made in the first instance today, the child can practically leave the borders of Poland tomorrow. We introduce the principle that in order for a child to leave the borders of Poland, there must be a valid judgment. This is an absolutely important change. What you asked me about the Jugendamt case. I would like to say that a few weeks ago I had a conversation with a representative of the German Ministry of the Family, with Deputy Minister Kleindiek. We talked about the Jugendamts. We were interested in two things. The first is the operation of the so-called. a letter from Polish foster families in diplomatic institutions. The idea is that children who have Polish citizenship should not be placed in foster families during proceedings where they will not have the possibility of preserving their national identity, i.e. a language, in accordance with the UN-Ows Convention, in accordance with Article. 8, which speaks on this subject. This is the first thing: if the child is to be placed somewhere, it is in Polish families. Case number two. The aim is to ensure that the provisions of the Vienna Convention are respected, i.e. that representatives of diplomatic services participate at every stage, including enforcement, in proceedings before the German institutions. There was openness. I must say, Minister, among others. led [[♪ The bell ♪]] to a meeting held several days ago in Germany, with representatives of the Jugendamts, all in Germany. It was an important meeting during which a representative of the Ministry could present our rations. We're taking this case very seriously. I would like to inform you that the project will soon be submitted. He is already in the process of inter-ministerial consultations, he is already after the conference and will be submitted to the Committee of the Permanent Council of Ministers, then to the Council of Ministers, and will soon be in Parliament. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u34	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! With regard to the questions raised by Members of the Prosecutor General, the Minister for Justice, I would like to emphasise first of all that, in line with the wording of the article. 521 § 1 Code of Criminal Procedure Prosecutor General - Minister of Justice may bring an appeal to the Supreme Court against any final judgment of the court terminating the proceedings, and this is the case in this case. Cassation is an exceptional appeal which can only be brought because of shortcomings which are so-called. the absolute reasons for appeal or other gross infringement if it could have had a significant impact on the content of the judgment. In this respect, i.e. in the light of the finding of grounds for expropriation from final judgments of the courts terminating proceedings, the file of cases in which such final decisions have been issued shall be examined in the Department of the Court of Justice of the National Public Prosecutor's Office. This shall be done either by its own motion or at the request of the parties to the proceedings and their procedural agents, as well as by other eligible entities, such as the Presidents of the courts, the Chairs of the departments in the courts, or Members or Senators who intervene in the performance of their duties in the bodies and bodies referred to in Article. Article 20 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Act on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator. Examination of the final completion of criminal and criminal proceedings often results in cassation in cases brought before the prosecutor general and taken out of office. For example, in 2016 195 cancellations were brought from final judgments of the courts closing proceedings, and in this year until 7 June - 110 cancellations. The number of exceptional appeals may indicate that a significant proportion of the final judgments of the courts are affected by the assessment of the public prosecutor-general and have an impact on the content of the decision in breach of the law. However, it should also be noted that in the other cases examined, and such is the majority, the grounds for referring an exceptional appeal to the Supreme Court have not been established. However, in any situation where such a statement is made, it shall be triggered on the basis of the applicable rules of cassation proceedings, which is the only legally regulated possibility for the Minister of Justice, the Prosecutor-General, to intervene in the law-impaired final judicial decisions. It is only by means of an appeal that the Attorney General may express an assessment of such a judgment in a particular case under examination, including an assessment of the court's behaviour during the examination of evidence of objectivity and impartiality at the hearing. The question raised by the Members of the Court of Justice of 6 June 2017 has not yet been examined by the District Court of Choszczno in the National Public Prosecutor's Office for the possibility of expropriation from the end of the proceedings of the final judgment of the court, since none of the parties to the proceedings requested this. Nor did we obtain from the office information that the final judgment issued in it was affected by a flagrant and significant influence on its content in breach of substantive law or procedural law. In view of the content of the question made by Members and the issues raised within them, it has now been decided to initiate the actions described under Article. 521 § 1 of the Code of Criminal Procedure. However, it will only be possible to take a position on the correctness and validity of the final judgment of the District Court in Szczecin, after a thorough examination of the material of the procedure. On the other hand, the preliminary findings, which were only possible during the current period, show that the proceedings in the case of the District Court in Choszczno initiated by the Indictment Act of the District Public Prosecutor's Office in Szczecin of 28 December 2006, in which the indictment was filed, inter alia. against Rafał T. and Grzegorz K., was placed under the official supervision of the then Prosecutor's Office of Appeal in Szczecin. By judgment of the District Court of Choszczno of 24 November 2015, the defendants were found guilty of committing the acts referred to in Article. 282 Criminal Code and Art. 13 § 1 of the Penal Code in conjunction with Article. 282 The Penal Code, which is commonly referred to as the so-called. Robbery extortion to the detriment of the victim and his family. The legal right to appeal against this judgment was exercised by the defendants and their defenders, as well as by the defendant, his attorney and the prosecutor. Following the judgment of 1 February 2016 in Szczecin, the Regional Court of Szczecin partially amended the judgment of the Court of First Instance, including as regards the extent to which the sentence was imposed against the defendants Rafał T. and Grzegorz K., which was increased to 5 years and 5 and 6 months of imprisonment respectively. The District Attorney in Szczecin found this final judgment of the Court of Appeal to be valid. In conclusion, and at the same time referring to the fundamental issue which is the subject of the Members' inquiry, I must state that, for the time being, there is no reason to believe that the judgment in question is defective. Its correctness and validity, as I said earlier, can only be claimed after an examination of the file, which will take place immediately. However, it should be stressed that even when the examination of the case file would show shortcomings in the assessment of the evidence collected in it by the court, it is not possible to carry out an overall assessment of the objectivity of the case law in Poland on this individual case alone. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u117	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u118	I ask the Minister to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u119	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! There are already solutions in the current legal system that protect local government entities from liquidity loss, solvency and are aimed at preventing uncontrolled debt growth. I want to talk about two, given the time constraints. The two most important are, firstly, the principle of balancing the budget. And here we are talking about the fact that the government at the planning stage of the budget is obliged to respect the principle of balancing the budget, which means that it cannot adopt a budget in which the planned current expenditure is higher than the planned current revenue plus the budget surplus from previous years and free resources. Local authorities are also required to comply with this principle at the end of the financial year. The second rule is to limit the possibility of borrowing debt. This is where the cap on the debt of local government units, as set out in the Public Finance Act in Article, has been applied since 2014. 243. The individual debt repayment rate is designed, among other things, to be used. Secure local government units from excessive debt levels, because this limit depends on the economic situation of the local government and also on excessive accumulation of debt payments in relation to the economic capacity of the local government. At present, repayment of loan instalments and debt support for a given financial year must not exceed the arithmetic average of the operating surplus technically calculated as the difference between current revenue and current expenditure plus revenue from the sale of assets of the three preceding years. The level of current expenditure is most affected by local self-government units themselves, as they are obliged to have a responsible, reliable, rational and economic financial policy. The design of the indicator, which limits the repayment of a local government unit's debt, does not limit the absorption of European funds for projects supported by these funds. The individual debt repayment rate is monitored and analysed by the Ministry of Finance on an ongoing basis. The financial situation of local government units shall be assessed on the basis of the data contained in the reports drawn up by them and in the multiannual financial forecast. I would also like to say that on the basis of the analyses carried out and the conclusions of the supervisory authorities, i.e. The Regional Audit Chamber, as well as the Supreme Audit Chamber, as well as the monitoring carried out by the Minister of Finance, have prepared an amendment to the Public Finance Act, which provides for, inter alia. increase the financial security of local government units. With regard to the draft law on the amendment of the Public Finance Act, which is currently being processed, where we have gathered comments and are currently analysing them, two solutions are assumed. The first option is to allow in practice the restructuring of the debt of local government units by repaying existing debt with new debt with lower costs. Thus, as a result of the local government units that have entered into, I would say more expensive commitments, and in the course of the repayment of this debt they have the opportunity to convert this debt into a more favourable one, of course as regards the cost of service, this is the possibility here. Secondly, early repayment of the debt in a situation of own funds. This proposal will allow the financial economy of the local government to be rationalised while reducing public debt and the cost of handling it. The second solution we have proposed in the Act is to strengthen the legal mechanisms to increase the financial security of local government units, among others. as a response to the results of the checks that I am calling for here today, i.e. taking into account, when calculating the individual debt repayment rate, those liabilities which have similar or equivalent economic effects to the loan or credit agreement. And here we are thinking above all about non-standard financing instruments. This will eliminate some circumvention, I would say, the debt ratio and thus increase the financial security of local government units. According to the Regional Audit Chamber, financial liabilities, which show a significant similarity to the financial consequences of credit agreements or loans, occurred in 290 local government units for a total amount of nearly PLN 1.5 billion. The inclusion of commitments that have economic effects similar to the loan or loan agreement, a reduction such as for loans or traditional loans, will prevent uncontrolled debt growth in the area of local government units. The current requirement for an opinion by the Regional Audit Chamber is also reinforced by the fact that we require a positive opinion on the ability of the entity to repay its debt. And it will be an additional mechanism to prevent excessive debt. This will strengthen the process of monitoring the debt of local government units and contribute to increased financial security. Here is one more thing - excluding free resources as a rule regarding the sustainability of the budget, as defined in [[♪ The bell ♪]] Public Finance Act in Article. 242 so that the entities do not incur excessive credit. I can say that it is now the case that self-government units are deliberately borrowing higher credits, which under this rule are added to income to generate higher expenditure. These practices are indicated by the NIK reports as well as by the Regional Audit Chamber. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u131	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, To answer the question about... In fact, I think that two questions really concern one fundamental problem, namely how to prevent the loss of socio-economic functions by those areas in Poland that lost them despite their accession to the European Union, despite the first wave of structural measures that have significantly affected the development of major areas, mainly metropolitan areas, mainly voivodship cities, although we often point out that not all these cities, not all 16 voivodship cities have developed evenly over the past years. Indeed, the dominant model, the so-called one. polarizing and diffusion, has greatly influenced the development of the largest agglomerations in Poland. They are today's development lever, but the diffusion is not happening, i.e. powiats located in a significant distance from centres in which the relevant road or rail infrastructure has not yet been developed, are doomed to marginalisation. She mentioned that the disparities in the Mazowieckie voivodship after accession had increased dramatically. Before accession the difference between Warsaw and Radom and Pułtusk was two times, and today it is four times. This does not result from the fact that Pułtusk was not developing at all, but from the fact that most of the funds were actually located in Warsaw. Hence, in the Responsible Development Strategy, the response to this identified problem, dedicated to the so-called. medium-sized cities. There is also a distinction for them, which we have prepared on the basis of an analysis of the Polish Academy of Sciences, where we have distinguished a group of 255 medium-sized cities. We have many criteria, not only large, but if we're talking about size, they're cities over 20,000. residents or cities between 15 and 20,000, which are the capital of the county. This group, as I say, is still divided into two groups of cities, which are particularly affected by unemployment, whether lack of infrastructure or depopulation. There are 120 cities in this group of 255 cities. Public intervention will be undertaken in a special way in relation to these 120. What are the instruments gonna be? The first recruitments under all horizontal programmes, Eastern Poland Operational Programme, Infrastructure and Environment, Knowledge, education, development, as well as PO IR, Operational Programme, Intelligent Development started last week. This causes special, special preferences for those cities that can not only... Because what's the diagnosis at the same time? That at this point, we're not just about building roads and bridges. This is of course absolutely important, but this does not guarantee that these cities will not lose their social function, because losing their social function means e.g. the lack of attractive jobs, and the road is not a fundamental lever for such jobs to be created. Hence, this multi-purpose support programme, within which we dedicated not only European funds, by releasing this special financing line for these cities, but also in cooperation with the Polish Development Fund and established under the Fund for Local Government Investments, was set up an instrument to support large strategic investments for these cities. Our experience also shows that not only are resources the key to success, but also the ability to create good projects in these cities. Therefore, the Polish Fund has already started the first training and meetings with the authorities of these cities, also at the official level, with officials, so that they are ready to prepare good projects with a good, prudent financial assembly. Furthermore, we are aware that, as I said, the key is not only infrastructure, this hard road, rail infrastructure, but also attracting good, attractive investments. Hence the process of amending the Act on Special Economic Zones, which at the moment ended in the Ministry of Development, which gives special preferences to investors who will want to stay in the zones, and provides even more aid, to the greater extent of public aid to those who want to place their investments in these 250 localities. Furthermore, we are aware that we are today 2.5 years before the end of the current financial perspective. Negotiations are ongoing with the European Commission on the next plan and Structural Funds. In these discussions, we also dedicate special interest, as I said, to those areas which are in a clear development gap, and we are trying to negotiate in such a way that a sustainable support instrument is also enshrined in this next perspective after 2020. This is very important support, because on today's map of public aid many of these towns could not reach for large public aid because they are in this voivodship, and placing them on this map gives the opportunity to reach for additional support. Therefore, both advice and broadly understood investment in hard infrastructure, [[♪ The bell ♪]] in educational programs - this is something that is a program addressed to medium-sized cities. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u220	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights of the meeting held on 2 June 2017, at which the initiative of the President of the Republic was examined, a draft law amending the Law on proceedings before administrative courts. From a legal point of view, this is a small initiative, because it is only two articles, but it concerns cases relating to four articles in the Law on Administrative Courts, namely Article. 28, Art. 175, Art. 194 and Article 19 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). 287, except that the reason for this initiative is the entry into force of the provisions of the Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland of 15 December 2016. The Prosecutor General may take over the representation in proceedings before administrative courts at the reasoned request of a governmental authority, an organisational unit without legal personality or an entity representing the Treasury. The provisions of the Article shall apply. Paragraph 7 5 and 6 shall apply mutatis mutandis. The replacement shall be carried out by the Public Prosecutor General in proceedings before administrative courts in an optional manner. According to Art. 10, which I have already mentioned, the Act on the Prosecutor General's Prosecutor's Office may, in specific judicial proceedings, take over a replacement at the request of one of the entities of a governmental authority, an organisational unit without legal personality or an entity representing the State Treasury. This provision therefore does not directly correspond to whose replacement the Prosecutor General may take over, i.e. by using the terminology of the Law of 30 August 2002. The law on proceedings before administrative courts for which entities acting in administrative proceedings of the Prosecutor General may take action. The answer to this question requires that Article be taken into account. 25 Law on proceedings before administrative courts and articles. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. Point 1 (4) of the Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office. Based on these provisions, we can conclude that the Attorney General may take over the replacement of government administrations which, as falling within the category of public administrations, have judicial capacity under Article. 25 § 1 Law on proceedings before administrative courts. Used in art. The 10th Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office covers the concept of an organisational unit without legal personality as indicated in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 point 4 of that law State organisational units without legal personality. Following the entry into force of the Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office on 1 January 2017, the provisions of the Law on proceedings before administrative courts have been amended, in particular Article. 28 and Article 28 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, However, these changes need to be completed. And so in the article. 28 of that Law by adding the second sentence of § 2 It has been taken into account that the Attorney General may take action for the State Treasury in judicial proceedings. However, it has not been taken into account that in accordance with Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. Point 1 (4) in conjunction with Article. The 10th Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Public Prosecutor General may also act before administrative courts for governmental authorities and for state organisational units without legal personality. The second major amendment concerns Article. 175. With effect from 1 January 2017, it was amended § 2 by indicating that the solicitor's obligation under § 1 to draw up a cassation complaint does not apply in a case in which the prosecutor of the General Prosecutor's Office is a party, a representative or a proxy, but does not act on the basis of a power of attorney but on the basis of a statutory mandate pursuant to Article. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Act on the Attorney General. Therefore, in order to avoid doubt, it is proposed to Article. 175 plus § 2a of such content: actions in the proceedings for governmental authorities, state organisational units without legal personality or the Treasury shall be taken by the Public Prosecutor General. As a result, there will be no doubt that it is indicated in the article. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office, Vice-Presidents and Counsels of the Public Prosecutor's Office, who are to be removed from the list of lawyers or have a suspended right to pursue the profession of legal counsel, may file cassation complaints in judicial administrative proceedings in cases in which the replacement has been taken over by the Public Prosecutor's Office. Another major change concerns the repeal of the Article. 287 Law on proceedings before administrative courts. In accordance with that provision, where the court in the judgment revokes the contested decision and the authority, when examining the case, will again cancel the proceedings or if the court annuls the act or establishes a legal obstacle to the annulment of the act, the party who has suffered the damage shall serve as compensation from the authority which issued the decision. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 287 The law on proceedings before administrative courts in the current state of law is a relic of rules based on the provisions repealed by the Law of 17 June 2004 amending the Civil Code Act and certain other articles. 160 The Code of Administrative Procedure, which introduced the responsibility of the authorities for issuing a defective administrative decision, with this compensation being limited to actual damage and there was no principle of full damage, is real damage plus lost benefits, namely the Latin Damnum Emergens and Lucrum Assans. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 287 The law on proceedings before administrative courts was not adapted to the code regulation at that time, since, let me remind you, the code contains a stand-alone basis for damages - it is a matter of Article. 417 § 2 Civil Code - which is the sole basis for responsibility for decisions or other administrative acts. This draft law does not contain a transitional provision because it is redundant in this situation. As far as entry into force is concerned, there is no vacathio legis either. The law shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication. This is simply dictated by the need to protect the interests of the Treasury. These interests are being protected by the Attorney General, because the possible replacement of the representatives of the Attorney General in these proceedings which are already under way will, in this situation, rely only on the presentation of the power of attorney, if this procedure is already under way, e.g. from those units that I have already mentioned, and in particular may concern government administrations which will order prosecutions by the Public Prosecutor-General, and state organisational units that do not have legal personality. Thank you very much. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I request that this legislative initiative of the President of the Republic be continued.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u222	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the draft law of the President of the Republic of Poland amending the Law on proceedings before administrative courts, print No 1563. From 1 January this year. The Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland, which amended the provisions of the Law on proceedings before administrative courts, entered into force. In practice, these changes, in particular concerning Article. 28 and Article 28 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, The main objective of the draft law is to remove doubts in the application of the law by clarifying the changes made and ensuring its consistency. According to the Act, the Public Prosecutor General may take over the representation in proceedings before administrative courts at the request of one of the following entities: government administration, organizational unit without legal personality, entity representing the Treasury. The replacement of the Attorney General is always optional. Amendment to Article 28 introduced on 1 January this year. takes into account only that the Public Prosecutor's Office may act as the State Treasury, but disregards the possibility of replacing government administrations and state organisational units without legal personality. This obvious inconsistency requires a change, which is made in the procedated bill. Another inaccuracy is created in the article. 175 The law on proceedings before administrative courts, which states that the solicitor's obligation to draw up a cassation complaint is not applicable in a case in which the Attorney General is a party, a representative or a proxy. In the meantime, the Attorney General's Attorney General is not acting on the basis of a power of attorney but on the basis of a statutory mandate. In order to avoid doubt, the provisions in Article have been extended. 175 Rights of legal proceedings before administrative courts, which clearly and precisely govern these matters. In addition, the draft law repeals the article. 286 The rights of proceedings before administrative courts, which introduced the possibility of obtaining compensation from the authority which issued the decision, where the court in the judgment revokes the contested decision and the authority re-examines the case, cancels the proceedings or annuls the act or establishes a legal obstacle to the annulment of the act. The repeal of this Article in practice means that damages will only be dealt with on the basis of Article. 417 Civil Code, which is already a basic legal act in matters of compensation. The changes made to the Law on proceedings before administrative courts do not give rise to substantive doubts, but the errors and ambiguities created are the result of excessive haste in the examination of projects at all stages of legislation and lack of due care for the quality of the law. Unfortunately, this has already become a rule of procedure, to the detriment of the Polish legal system. It is unacceptable to propose no vacathio legis. The justification for the proposal is the need to remove doubts as soon as possible, and it has been almost six months since these uncertainties exist. This is not a convincing justification. According to the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, implementation of the bill requires at least a 14-day vacatio legis and I make such an amendment on behalf of the club. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u226	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club Modernly, I am presenting my position on the presidential draft law amending the Law on Procedure in Administrative Courts. There is no doubt that really the work on this law was carried out on the wrong side, among others. There are therefore errors in the law on proceedings before administrative courts. Two essential elements have not been taken into account. In principle, this law aims to clarify the Article. 28 and Article 28 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 175 Law on proceedings before administrative courts in such a way as to ensure that the General Prosecutor's Office can fully represent the State Treasury in these matters. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u234	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the club of the Polish People's Party on the report of the commission on the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the amendment of the Law on proceedings before administrative courts. According to the Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland, it replaces the Polish State, among others. The Treasury and government authorities, in proceedings before courts, tribunals and other judicial authorities. The Attorney General is also supposed to provide them with access to professional legal assistance. The aim of this project is to adapt the law on proceedings before administrative courts to the Act of 15 December 2016 on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland in force from 1 January 2017. [[OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. U. poz. 2261]] and the scope of the entity's operation resulting from the above Act. The Presidential project assumes that, within the scope of a specific Act on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland, the prosecutor may act before administrative courts also for governmental authorities and for state organisational units without legal personality. This scope of action of the Public Prosecutor General of the Republic of Poland stems from the regulation of the Act on the Public Prosecutor General of the Republic of Poland, but does not stem directly from the current content of the article. 28 Rights of proceedings before administrative courts. Proposed amendment to Article 175 The law on proceedings before administrative courts consists in the addition of a regulation confirming that the requirement for a cassation action by a lawyer or legal adviser does not apply also when actions in proceedings for governmental authorities, for state organisational units without legal personality and for the Treasury are taken by the Public Prosecutor General of the Republic of Poland. Such a change is advisable in order to avoid doubts as to the possible scope of action taken by the Attorney General of the Republic of Poland. The last proposed amendment is to repeal the regulation of the Article. 287 Rights of proceedings before administrative courts. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 287 of the Act in its current legal state is a remnant of the rules based on the repealed Article. 160 Code of Administrative Procedure. Art. 287 The law on proceedings before administrative courts was supplemented by the provision of the Article. 160 c.p.a. providing for the responsibility of the authorities issuing the defective decision - another administrative act - and was a logical complement to it. At present, the question of liability is governed by Article. 417 § 2 Civil Code. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 287 The law on proceedings before administrative courts has not been adapted to the abovementioned. only the reference to the repealed Article has been deleted. 160 c.p.a. In this situation, since the provision of the article. 417 § 2 c.c. is the sole basis for the responsibility for decisions - other administrative acts, Article. 287 Rights before administrative courts must be considered redundant. Removal of the provision of the Article. 287 of the above law will ensure consistency of the Polish civil law system and will increase legal security for victims of decisions and other administrative acts. High Izbo! The proposed amendment is intended to ensure consistency between civil and administrative law and undoubtedly regulates the law on proceedings before administrative courts as regards the presence by the Public Prosecutor-General on behalf of government administrations and public organisational units. This will avoid doubts as to the possible scope of the lawyers of the Attorney General of the Republic of Poland. Therefore, it appears justified to refer the project to further work.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u325	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The aim of the proposed law on amending the Act on subscription fees and the Act on Radiophony and Television, contained in Seym printing No 1587, is to improve the efficiency of the collection of subscription fees as a source of funding for the implementation of public broadcasting missions. The Act does not introduce any new fees for any group of citizens. As is the case at present, each holder of a radio or television receiver who is ready to receive the emissions must and will be obliged to register receivers and pay subscription fees. The proposed regulation is intended only to ensure a more adequate, stable and independent financing of public media tasks so that these tasks can be carried out without commercial pressure. The need to adopt the proposed law is due to the practical dysfunction of existing solutions regarding subscription fees. It is proposed to counter this by amending the Act on subscription fees including television service providers to facilitate the registration of radio and television receivers and the identification of households obliged to pay subscription fees. What is the current status of these subscription fees in Poland? Currently, the definition of subscription fees is regulated in the Act, they are of a specific public tribute. It is a compulsory, non-refundable, targeted and non-budget public-law service for the fulfilment of the State's constitutional tasks in financing the public media mission. The Act combines them with the use of radio and television receivers on the presumption that a person with a receiver is using this receiver immediately. For the purposes of charging subscription fees, receivers shall be registered under an administrative penalty of 30 times a monthly fee. It's all in the bill so far. One of the main reasons for the dysfunction of the existing subscription fee collection system is failure to comply with the obligation to register receivers. In conjunction with the lack of real means of controlling use, having receivers in residential premises above. the situation is conducive to leaks in the system. Since 1994, there has been a steady decrease in the number of registered radio and television subscribers. In 1993, there were almost 11 million of them, including about 1 million of them exempt from the payment of charges, while in 2015 there were less than 7 million registered subscribers, including slightly more than 3 million of them released. In Poland there are some 13.6 million households. According to CUS data in about 97% from which there are television sets, and approx. 66% households use pay-TV services, including cable, satellite, IPTV digital platforms, etc. Meanwhile, the obligation to register receivers complements 49.5% households, of which 47.2% he's entitled to layoffs. It is estimated that households regularly paying subscription fees in Poland, after deduction of late payments and claims received by execution, are around 13%. So only 13%. households pay their subscription regularly. Subscription fees for non-domestic receivers - business and institutions - regulate about 12% entities. As a result, Poland has the largest loss of subscription fees among European countries. In other countries, losses in the collection of similar charges are usually not more than a few percent. With us, we estimate, because it depends on what measure we're going to apply, this loss is certainly more than 50%, most likely 60%. As a result of the dysfunction of the subscription fee collection system in Poland, public funding of public media is unstable and insufficient, which leads to the commercialisation of the program offer, which is detrimental to the execution of the public mission. On the scale of the whole public media system, fluctuations are evident, and in the long term there is a decrease in the funds transferred to these broadcasters from subscription fees. The missionary activity of the public radio station is financed in approx. 60-80% of the subscription, while the costs of the mission of the Polish Television - only in approx. 30%. As a result, the share of public funds in the public television budget is the lowest in Europe in Poland. For years it has been pointed out that the level of public funding of Polish public media is insufficient in relation to the fulfilment of their statutory tasks. The situation is all the more serious and urgent that, according to the KRRiT's 2017 forecast, the cost of the TVP mission from the subscription is to continue to fall until the level of approx. 22% , and in the case of Polish Radio and regional radio stations - up to 66% and 71% respectively. Public media expenditure in Poland is low against other European countries, also in the light of general economic indicators. For example, while in 2013 public media revenues In several European countries surveyed, the average was 0.23% GDP, this share in Poland was 0.04% GDP. General assumptions for the proposed amendments. It is proposed to improve the collection of subscription fees by including pay-TV service providers in the process of facilitating the registration of receivers and the identification of entities required to pay subscription fees. It is particularly gross if there is no registration and payment of subscription fees when such services are used, when it is obvious that the receiver is not in possession of the receiver. This is because there is often a particular mix of remuneration for the private service of providing paid television, wrongly referred to as subscription, and public tribute, which is a subscription and which is a subscription fee for the performance of public broadcasting missions. It should be noted that the basis for the subscription fees - I recall, at the basis of the law already in force - is the assumption that the whole society in the Community is benefiting from the public broadcasting of its public service mission. Therefore, the system of fees allocated for this purpose in Poland and in other countries is not dependent on whether the citizen is using public media programs and services or using other media services. These fees are not equivalent. Just because someone pays for satellite or cable TV doesn't mean they pay their subscription automatically. Subscription fees correspond to the general nature of public media services and their role in the exercise of the right to information, access to cultural goods or participation in the social debate. The proposed amendment does not propose an obligation on pay-TV service providers to collect subscription fees, but merely entrust them with a purely informative obligation to provide basic data to persons with whom they have contracts for the provision of paid-TV services. Within them, a pay-TV service provider would be required to inform the so-called. the designated operator, in this case Poland Post, as has been the case so far, of any new contract it has concluded for the provision of pay-TV, and the designated operator would be entitled to request information about subscribers from the pay-TV service provider in order to determine the entity required to pay the subscription fee, its collection or execution. When concluding the contract, the pay-TV provider would also be required to provide the customer with information about the obligation to register the receiver and to pay subscription fees. So it's just a matter of passing on information. The customer would be informed that some of his personal data will be transferred to the designated operator. I repeat, I stress, I will continue to talk about this - only some personal data. The transitional regulation also provides for a one-time transmission by the television provider to the designated operator of information about the recipients of his services also in the above mentioned. the purpose and the transmission by him to customers of the presumption that the contract for the supply of pay-TV is linked to the possession of a television receiver which is subject to registration. The pay-TV service provider will also be required to inform its customers in the above mentioned. a communication that it will transmit certain personal data to the designated operator. The pay-TV service provider will also be required to submit registrations if the customer decides to register the receiver via this provider. Registration through a pay-TV service provider will therefore only be a customer's right. It's not gonna be a warrant-- he's gonna be able to use it or he's not. Further activities related to the recording of receivers, collection of fees, subscription control and verification of exemptions will be carried out by the designated operator, i.e. so far - by the Polish Post. Specific solutions. The Act introduces a presumption of possession of a television receiver by a person who is a party to a contract for the supply of paid television. This presumption is justified by the fact that it would not be advisable to conclude a contract with a pay-TV service provider without having a receiver to use its services. The proposed regulation envisages defining key concepts for the proposed solutions, including the concept of a pay-TV service provider and the concept of a contract for providing this service. Such a service would be provided by operators distributing television programmes - not e.g. sender of coded paid programmes - providing access to packages on the basis of contracts, not e.g. operators of open terrestrial digital television multiplex. At present, the definition of pay-TV service providers will include operators such as cable network operators, digital TV platform operators, xDSL programme distribution service providers and IPTV services - these are new types of cable, new types using new ICT technologies. So we limit this scope to entities that meet a certain definition. There are already allegations in public space that e.g. This does not apply to the use of video on demand or to digital terrestrial television. The law often has to be adapted to reality. We have decided on such a range of services and such a definition of the entities that we ask for cooperation in order to achieve the purpose of this law as is possible from the point of view of the legal tool used. As indicated above, the Act maintains the obligation to register a radio or television receiver. The receiver holder will be able to perform this obligation via Polish Post or pay-TV service provider. It's his decision. The absence of registration via a pay-TV service provider will not affect the validity of the contract for the provision of pay-TV or the manner in which the service is provided by the provider. However, the person who does not register the receiver at the post office or via the supplier will be, but exactly as is currently the case, subject to a fee for the use of an unregistered receiver of 30 times the subscription fee. So we're not introducing any new punishment either. This is a punishment that results from the existing law. The Act proposes improving control mechanisms. And this is the main purpose of this law, as I said - sealing the system, which simply does not work in Poland. In the first place, pay-TV providers will be required to provide information on any contract for the provision of pay-TV and basic information concerning the person with whom such a contract has been concluded. As a result, the designated operator, i.e. Poczta Polska, will be kept informed of the persons who should register the receiver and pay the subscription fee. In addition, the operator appointed by law to collect licence fees shall be given competence to request information from pay-TV service providers on the persons using these services. Similarly, pay-TV operators will also carry out a basic service or, in short, according to legal terminology, will carry out a public task. Such public tasks are imposed on non-governmental, non-public institutions by many other laws in force in Poland and it is nothing special, it does not break the legal order and is legally lege artis. Such a request would be individual, i.e. it would be a question and relate to individual persons or addresses. The designated operator will not be able to address pay-TV service providers with a general request for all customers or groups thereof. Each request from the designated operator shall be for a single customer. Where a designated operator is informed of any contract concluded for the provision of pay-TV, it is assumed that a request for information from a pay-TV provider would be addressed only in exceptional cases, e.g. if the receiver is not registered by the person who had a contract to provide pay-TV or if it is otherwise established that there are receivers at the address of the person, company or institution. It is necessary to obtain such data in order to determine the entity required to pay the licence fee, its collection or enforcement. Without these data, the designated operator would not have been able to proceed with the collection of subscription fees from persons who have unregistered radio or television receivers. The scope of the data provided to the designated operator also meets the adequacy requirement, i.e. proportionality of data. Pay-TV providers have much broader customer databases, including not only address data but also contract details, packages and programs preferred by the client, transaction history or payment information. These data are necessary for the operation of pay-TV service providers but do not have any effect on the obligation to pay subscription fees. And we're not interested in the data in this bill. The collection of only part of the data held by the pay-TV service provider cannot therefore be considered as copying of the data sets. They will be obliged to provide from a significant number of data only part of the information about their clients which is necessary for the establishment of the entity required to pay the subscription fee, its collection or execution. The information obligation would only be relevant data, i.e. the name and address of the customer and the address of the customer, the address of use of the service and the start date of use of the service or the PESEL number, and, where there is no PESEL, the identity card number, NIP or KRS, depending on what is available, what is included in the contract. In addition, the request for information and the corresponding obligation of the provider to provide information will apply only to customers using pay-TV services and not to other services provided by its provider, e.g. telephone or Internet access, because they are not related to having a receiver ready to receive. It should be stressed that the transmission of the data to the designated operator will also not constitute a breach of the company's secrecy. The protection would be subject to bases containing all information exchanged between the trader and the customer where their transfer could be used for competitive activities by the entity receiving the data. The project essentially limits the number of personal data, as I mentioned, transferred to the designated operator. I repeat, therefore, there will be no transmission of other data contained in the databases of pay TV providers, such as details of contracts, packages, preferred programs, etc., transaction history or information on the amount of payments. The designated operator will only be able to use the data received for the purpose of charging subscription fees. There is therefore no risk that it will use this data to compete with pay-TV providers. Especially since Poczta Polska does not carry out such activities. The project aims to allow the use of the data obtained only for a specific purpose - the collection of public-law tributes. Information on contracts for the provision of pay-TV and on persons with whom such contracts have been concluded is expected to be provided via an electronic system to be created by the designated operator. This system will include a register of people with a radio or television receiver and will allow the provider of pay-TV services to send the above. information. In view of the fact that the designated operator pays a commission on the licence fees collected, it is justified that it is obliged to set up such a system and then to make it available free of charge to pay-TV service providers. This system will have to ensure the security of personal data and comply with the requirements set out in the Regulation based on the IT Act governing the activities of public entities. The Minister responsible for Communications will control the functioning of the system. By providing personal data to the designated operator, the pay-TV service provider will be required to inform the customer concerned. The consent of each customer of the pay-TV provider will not be required to transmit data to the designated operator. In accordance with the Personal Data Protection Act, the processing of data is permitted without the consent of the data subject if it is necessary to exercise the right or to fulfil the obligation under the law. And this is the case in the proposed regulation, so that the various types of allegations that already appear in public space as a backup, concerning this proposed amendment, and that this is contrary to the Personal Data Protection Act, have no legal basis. Polish law in specific cases arising precisely from the law allows for the transfer of part of the data in an appropriate manner and under appropriate conditions. High Seym! All obligations imposed on pay-TV providers provided for in the proposed amendment of the Act as generating costs on their part will entail compensation for them. This compensation will be based on costs incurred by suppliers, but will also include a reasonable profit - not less than 6% and not more than 10% costs incurred. The KRRiT will, by means of a regulation, determine the amount of compensation and the method and method of payment thereof. This compensation will be paid from the proceeds of subscription fees collected by the Polish Post Office. We are counting on this issue, in general on the implementation of this law, to cooperate with pay-TV operators. At the same time, it is proposed to modify the rules for establishing compensation for the performance of public tasks for the designated operator. Currently, remuneration is determined by means of an agreement concluded by the Polish Post with the National Radio and Television Council. It is proposed that compensation for the designated operator be determined in the same way as compensation for pay-TV service providers. The Act will indicate that the designated operator is entitled to compensation for the provision of certain public services and that the amount and method of payment will be specified in the KRRiT Regulation. A review of the performance of the pay-TV provider's obligations regarding the collection of registration declarations as well as the provision of information on persons who have concluded a contract for the supply of pay-TV, in response to individual requests from the designated operator, is proposed to entrust the National Radio and Television Council. This corresponds to the function of that authority as competent for the financing of public media missions in the public interest in radio and television, on which the watchman, in the light of Article, is responsible. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. 1 Constitution stands for the KRRiT. It shall also have competence with regard to the providers of pay-TV services in connection with the keeping of a register of distributed programmes by the Chairman of the KRRiT. As part of the control of the implementation of the obligations imposed on pay-TV service providers, the Chairman of the KRRiT will be able to request information from them to the extent necessary for such control. Violation of the obligation to provide information will be subject to penalties in the form of a financial administrative penalty imposed by an administrative decision by the Chairman of the CRRiT of up to 5% the revenue of the pay-TV service provider generated by the supply of pay-TV services in the previous tax year and, where the duration of the pay-TV service provider is less than the calendar year, the amendment assumes a fine of up to 500 000. zł. It is also proposed to impose sanctions for infringement by the pay-TV service provider of obligations relating to the transmission of registration declarations, as well as information about clients, in the form of a fine imposed by the KRRiT on the amount of PLN 500 for each infringement. Given that an infringement of these obligations may lead to an impossible non-registration of the receiver, which, if established, would be punishable by a penalty of 30 times the licence fee payable, the penalty proposed is adequate. As indicated above, the Act grants the designated operator access to data from certain public registers, e.g. the PESEL register, and the right to obtain information on customers of pay-TV service providers. It is therefore necessary to ensure adequate data protection. At the outset, it should be pointed out that the exercise of a public service task under the Act is an objective for which personal data can be processed to the extent necessary. There is no doubt that the collection of public danins, including subscription fees for public broadcasting missions, is precisely the task pursued for the public good. The provisions of the Personal Data Protection Act will apply to personal data collected and processed in connection with the collection of subscription fees. Given that not all data processed by the operator designated in connection with the collection of subscription fees will be personal data, and given the scale of activities in this area, it is appropriate to introduce a specific regulation on data security. These safeguards will be structural in the first place. The controller of personal data will be the designated operator. The tasks relating to the processing of personal data may be entrusted to a dedicated unit of the designated operator, which shall be indicated by means of a regulation of the Minister responsible for communication in agreement with the KRRiT. This separate unit, currently a Financial Service Centre of Polish Post, will process data obtained by means of control and access to data from the PESEL register and data about customers of paid TV services. An operator designated to safeguard data will appoint an information security administrator whose tasks are defined by the Personal Data Protection Act. In view of the operator's operation of the different activities, the indication of the separate entity, together with further regulations, will be used to prevent the use of data in activities other than subscription. The data will only be used to determine the entity required to pay the licence fee, its collection or enforcement. It will not be permitted to transfer data to other entities designated than the above-mentioned separate entity of the designated operator, as well as to entities other than those carrying out enforcement proceedings relating to the non-payment of subscription fees, and it will not be permitted to use them for purposes other than those set out in this Act. The processing of individual data by operators will be possible within a separate operator unit designated solely for the purpose of determining the entity required to pay the subscription fee, its collection or enforcement and using measures to ensure data security. In order to enhance the protection of data processed in the implementation of the Act, special protection of the related secrecy will be introduced. The subject of protection will be the individual data of the entities required to pay the licence fees and the entities exempt from them, obtained by the operator designated as a result of the implementation of the law. The entities subject to the obligation to keep this data confidential will be managers, employees and co-workers of the designated operator's separate organisational unit, as well as other employees and co-workers of the designated operator who are obtained in connection with business activities - e.g. persons employed in post offices - access to these data. The rules on postal secrecy will apply to this mystery. Point 4, transitional provisions. After the entry into force of this Act, pay-TV service providers will be required to provide the designated operator with information on each person who is a party to the contract for the supply of pay-TV once. The data communicated shall relate only to persons who have concluded contracts on the date of entry into force of the Act. It is proposed that customer information be provided within 60 days of the date of entry into force of the proposed law. This will enable suppliers to prepare themselves for this obligation. Suppliers will also be obliged to inform their customers of the obligation to register the receiver and pay subscription fees. These activities, like other obligations of pay-TV service providers provided for by the amendment, as I said, will be paid for. The amount of compensation will be determined in the same way as is the case with regard to other obligations of pay-TV service providers. The one-off obligations of pay-TV providers provided for in the draft transitional provisions must be subject to scrutiny. As with the new obligations of the project suppliers, the competent authority will be the KRRiT. The pay-TV service provider will be required to provide the KRRiT with a report on the performance of these duties within 6 months of the date of entry into force of the Act. It will only be statistical data, so it is not necessary to include their specific provisions on the protection of personal data. On the basis of the report, it will be possible to assess the correctness of the actions taken by the pay-TV service provider. On the basis of the information received, the KRRiT will be able to determine whether all persons have been informed of the new rules and whether the data of all persons required to pay the subscription fees will be made available to the designated operator. This information may also assess the effectiveness of the solutions adopted. The failure to report or the submission of an unreliable report shall be subject to the administrative penalty imposed by the Chairman of the CRR. In addition, the Act provides for abolition for persons who have registered the receiver, but who are behind with subscription fees. Currently, ladies and gentlemen, these arrears concern 2 million entities and 3 billion PLN. It is proposed that enforcement proceedings may be terminated against non-payment of subscription fees which, within 3 months of the date of entry into force of the Act, will fulfil their obligations, i.e. register the receiver if not registered, and pay the fee in advance for at least 6 months. In addition, it would not be possible for such persons to initiate new enforcement proceedings concerning arrears arising before the entry into force of the Law. Such a solution will lead to the remission of much greater amounts resulting from arrears than the amounts necessary to pay in order to obtain absolution. In order to guarantee and stress the lack of retroactivity of the proposed law, i.e. its non-retroactivity, it is also proposed to stress that it will not be possible to impose charges for the use of an unregistered receiver for the period prior to the date of entry into force of the law. It is proposed that the law should enter into force one month after the date of publication, except for the provision concerning the electronic system, which will enter into force within 3 months of the date of publication of the law, which is conditioned simply by the technical requirements for the construction of such a system. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above, I propose that the Sejm adopt a draft law amending the Act on subscription fees and the Act on Radiophony and Television contained in Seym printing No 1587. The project was the subject of extensive public consultation and inter-ministerial arrangements. The draft submitted shall take into account a significant proportion of the comments made under those arrangements. I would also like to stress that the proposal has received a positive opinion from the Legislative Council. The proposal will help to increase the level of public media funding in Poland, as well as to improve the quality of public service missions provided by public media. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u349	Mrs. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of Poselski, the Republicans have the honour to present their position on the Government's draft amendment to the Act on Subscription Fees and the Act on Radio and Television, as well as the Parliamentary bill amending the Act on Radio and Television and repealing the Act on Subscription Fees. What I would like to stress first is the fact that there is a significant violation here. Firstly, we are not dealing with any subscription here at all, because the subscription is, in accordance with the Commercial Companies Code, a form of settlement which is only possible if both parties to the civil law agreement accept the terms of the agreement and the agreement is signed by both parties. Only if this were the case would TVP be able to apply on the basis of such an agreement to fulfil this obligation. As far as I know, no owner of a radio-television receiver, generally speaking, has made such a contract, no holder of a television, no owner of a radio. Therefore, using the wording that this is a subscription, this is an important abuse and in this context should not be used. Secondly, I would like to protest against the justification put forward by the applicants that the transfer of data, the database of customers of cable networks is necessary for the fulfilment of obligations. This is not necessary because the form of collection could be different and more rational. It would not be necessary to require cable network owners to provide customer data, but simply to propose another form of collection, e.g. attach it to the electricity bill or simply turn it into a PIT. That's it. It would be simple and fair. Of course, only in a situation where the basic condition would be met, and therefore it would be television which is reliable, which is non-commercial, which is primarily focused on the execution of a public mission, to strengthen the Polish identity, to shape appropriate patriotic and civic attitudes, and not primarily on profit. If this mission is not put first, there is no need to charge any citizen an additional tax, because it is simply an ordinary tax, not any subscription. That's another line. What I would like to stress is the fact that today this law is aimed at ensuring that Poland Post simply gets additional databases of customers paid cable TV and, by increasing the database of these customers, can collect more fees and provide financing for public television. The important thing is that the principle of technological neutrality has also been violated in this solution, because we are dealing with favouritism of terrestrial television, for there is no need to conclude any additional agreements in the case of terrestrial television. Moreover, if we are to move towards the proposals proposed by the applicants, we should, for example: download bases from Central Vehicle and Driver Records, because every owner of a car usually has a radio. Then why aren't the CEPiK databases included in this bill? Of course, it would be a mistake, because I think that direction is wrong, and the bill in its assumptions is simply illogical. Another element that has already been highlighted by my predecessors, but I would also like it clearly [[♪ The bell ♪]] This is a lack of preparation of the Polish Post for the management of the new databases provided to it. Usually in every democracy it is, it is nothing special, so please do not use demagogic slogans that we are entering someone's privacy, etc. This can be done if this is necessary for the exercise of the right or the obligation under the law, and this is a quote of the law in force in Poland. As regards the company's secrecy, its purpose is to protect the trader's data against unfair competition. The full customer database can indeed be the secret of the company, but it should be remembered that Poland Post will receive only a small part of the customer information that is in possession of pay-TV providers. There will also be no risk of being used by an unfair competitor. Only Poczta Polska will have access to the data, which does not compete on the pay-TV market when providing access to the Internet, and the data received will be able to use only to a very narrow extent. The requirement to have a fair control over everyone is that everyone should have a controller over themselves is completely absurd. The control provisions shall be without prejudice to equality in respect of the law. All receiver owners are checked, only the means of this check differ. And that's what the bill is about. This law provides for the possibility of processing data without the consent of the person concerned, as I said, if this is necessary for the implementation of the law. One more point, because these specific legal issues, in my opinion, are important. Polish law provides for situations in which the change of law generally applicable results in the need to change the terms of civil law contracts, i.e. it is not so that the terms of civil law contracts cannot be changed even though such an agreement has been concluded. This is what Polish law provides, because reality is more complicated in many different dimensions, not only in the question of subscription. If we accept this bill, it will be possible, because it is possible. This issue has been extensively described, regulated by Article. 60a Telecommunication Law, which applies to contracts for the provision of pay-TV services. According to this regulation, where a change in the content of the contract, e.g. the rules governing the provision of services are directly due to the amendment of the law, the pay-TV service provider is obliged to notify the change of its client and to inform him of the right to terminate the contract. There are two conditions: we must inform you that the law has changed, and therefore our civil law agreement is already of a different nature and we must propose termination of the agreement. And that's fair, and that's legal, so please don't say that our amendment is illegal. This legality was confirmed by the clear views of the Legislative Council and, contrary to what Mrs GIODO said this time. The opinion on this project is generally positive. I will read the text of the draft resolution: The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the establishment of the year 2018 by the Year of Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk. Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk was born on 1 February 1918 near Szbarzy in the peasant family. In 1942, he graduated from the Conspiracy Seminary in Lviv and was ordained priestly. In the southeast of the second Republic he survived the Soviet and German occupation and the threat from Ukrainian nationalists. In 1946 he had to leave the Soviet Lviv again. In 1951 he received a PhD in philosophy at the Catholic University of Lublin. Ignacy Tokarczuk received a bishop in Przemyśl on February 6, 1966. At that time he started the program of sacral construction, thanks to which, despite the opposition of the communists, more than 400 new churches were established in the Przemysl diocese until 1993. He gained respect and devotion to the faithful by uncompromising sermons and creating new social ties in the Church. In the '70s. The 20th century bishop's curia in Przemyśl was a meeting place for democratic opposition and independence, including a particularly independent peasant movement. During the years of martial law, she assisted interned families and was an enclave of independent Poland, an independent culture and a support centre for Poles engaged in anti-communist activities. Bishop Ignacy Tokarczuk, as a strong opponent of the Soviet occupation of Poland, was fought fiercely, under surveillance and falsely accused against the faithful and church leaders. After Poland regained its independence, in 1992 he became an archbishop of the metropolitan region of Przemysl. In 2006, he was awarded the Order of the White Eagle in recognition of his outstanding merits in his work for the freedom of his homeland. Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk died on 29 December 2012 in Przemyśl. The Holy Father John Paul II wrote to the Archbishop: 'You have become to the Przemysl Church, as well as to the Church throughout Poland, a fearless witness of the Gospel truth, a steadfast defender of [...] the rights of believers to express their religious beliefs [...]. You were a spokesperson for a nation fighting for its sovereignty in a fight against the totalitarian state system. Your voice, Shepherd, was heard throughout the whole country, strengthened in spirit and gave courage when it was hard." The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, on the 100th anniversary of the birth of Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk - the metropolitan of Przemysl, the relentless defender of the rights of the faithful of the Catholic Church and one of the spiritual leaders of the peaceful struggles of the Polish nation for the independence of the Homeland, announces the year 2018 as the Year of Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I recommend that the High Sejm adopt a resolution on the establishment of 2018 as the Year of Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
