ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u480	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I have the honour to present a report on the draft resolution on the establishment of the year 2018 by the Year of the 250th anniversary of the Bar Confederation, prints 1513 and 1552. This project was submitted by the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering the draft resolution at its meeting on 24 May this year, during which the title of the resolution has been amended and several editorial amendments have been introduced, asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft resolution in the following wording. [[We know the content, sir.]] But it wouldn't hurt to quote it. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2018 as the Year of the Bar Confederation. In 2018 marks the 250th anniversary of the establishment of the Bar Confederation (1768-1772) - the armed union of the Polish nobility established in the Bar in Podole on 29 February 1768 in defence of the Catholic faith and independence of the Republic of Poland, the first great Polish national uprising. The purpose of the Confederation was to abolish the treaty imposed by Russia by which the Republic became a Russian protectorate and the laws adopted by the Russian forces of the Sejm, which gave equality to the inno-believers. The Act of the Confederation proclaimed: everywhere, to each and every individual, that the council, dignitaries, nobles and citizens, gathers to the rescue of the disgraced Homeland, faith, freedom of laws and national freedoms. The Barska Confederation lasted the longest of all uprisings and covered the largest area of the Republic of Poland. More than 100,000 fought on the Confederate side. People. Several hundred battles and skirmishes were fought. After the collapse of the Bar Confederation, some of its leaders remained in exile, several thousand prisoners were sent to Siberia, a large number of Confederates were incorporated into the Tsar army, and the propaganda of the neighbouring states tried to use this raid as one of the pretexts for the First Partition of Poland. The Barska Confederation, together with the Constitution on 3 May and the Kościuszko Uprising, entered the collective memory of Poles as examples of the struggle for freedom, which especially during the partitions was of great importance for the survival of the nation and which was most often referred to in the 19th century. Due to the works of the romantic wizards - Adam Mickiewicz, Juliusz Słowacki or Zygmunt Krasiński - the Confederates were presented as martyrs for their faith, freedom and homeland. Thanks to this, the Bar Confederation has become one of the breakthrough moments in the history of national consciousness and it is easy to find the distinctive features of all subsequent Polish breaks, until modern times. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland establishes 2018 as the Year of the Bar Confederation. He also believes that anniversary celebrations will contribute to the restoration of this first Polish national uprising in the defence of faith and freedom, which was removed from collective memory by communist historiography, its place in the history of our Nation. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u494	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I have the honour to present the committee's report on the draft resolution on the establishment of 2018 as the Year of Irena Sendler. According to Art. Article 32a (2) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm on 21 April this year. Marshal of the Sejm sent a draft resolution to the Committee on Culture and Media for first reading. The committee president held its first reading at its meeting on 25 May. The proposed amendments tabled by the members of the committee and the Legislative Bureau were taken into account during the meeting. Following the discussion, the members unanimously adopted the draft resolution. In this connection, the committee has given a positive opinion on this draft resolution and we are presenting it to the High House in the following wording, asking for its adoption: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2018 by Irena Sendlerova. Irena Sendlerova, nicknamed occupation 'Jolanta', is an outstanding Polish social activist. She was born on 15 February 1910 in Warsaw, where she died on 12 May 2008. During World War II, as head of the Children's Council for Jewish Aid, she organized a network of people and institutions with whom she helped children from the ghetto. She helped to save about 2500 Jewish children from the Holocaust. At risk of life, heroism, in the largest conspiracy she led children out of the Warsaw ghetto, and then found them shelter in Polish families, monasteries and nursing homes. In October 1943, Irena Sendlerov was arrested, interrogated and tortured at the Gestapo headquarters at Shucha Avenue and imprisoned for 100 days in Pawiak. Even under threat of death penalty, she did not betray her associates or reveal any information. Irena Sendler's record of rescued children allowed them to know their own identity, as well as to find loved ones after World War II. Irena Sendlerov was honored in 1965 with the Medal of the Righteous Among the Nations, in 2003 - the Order of the White Eagle, in 2007 - the Order of Smile. Since 1990 Honorary citizen of the State of Israel. On the 10th anniversary of the death of Irena Sendler, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to the Sejm, which with its greatest dedication worked to save the other man, and announces the year 2018 with the Year of Irena Sendler. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland dated... 2017 on the establishment of 2018 Year of Women's Rights. On 28 November 1918, Józef Piłsudski, Interim Chief of State, signed a decree on electoral ordination to the Legislative Sejm. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 of this decree states that every citizen of the State without gender difference is an electorate to the Sejm. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 7 It has been stated that all citizens and citizens of the State with active electoral rights are elected to the Sejm. This decision, confirmed in the March Constitution, was based on the Polish Republican tradition, in which women had legitimate subjectivity. The Reborn Republic has given them full voting rights. On the eve of the century of these events, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, establishing 2018 as the Year of Women's Rights, expresses its recognition to the first parliamentarians: Gabriela Balicka, Jadwid Dziubińska, Iren Kosmowska, Maria Moczydłowska, Zofia Moraczewska, Annie Piasecka, Zofia Sokolnicka and Franciszka Wilczkowakowa and all intercessors of the fight for equality. As a curiosity, I would like to add that for the first time women obtained electoral rights in New Zealand in 1893. They were reduced. In its entirety, the electoral rights were obtained in 1919. In Europe, for the first time in Finland in 1906 Poland, as we know, gave voting rights to women in 1918, France in 1944. There is one very unusual thing, namely in 1971... [[Switzerland.]] Yeah, you're right. ...in Switzerland, women were allowed to go to ballot boxes, but not all. In the canton of Appenzell Innerrhoden, the Swiss obtained their electoral rights only in 1990, in the same year as the women in Bangladesh. I ask you to pass this document because it is extremely important for our public awareness, political awareness, but above all for our Polish-Polish agreement. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

She didn't make it to the Sejm, she became the first lady, but she was still working for the state. I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media on the draft resolution on the celebration of the 80th anniversary of the start of the construction of the Central Industrial District, print No.154. The above draft resolution was tabled by Members of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, with the great commitment of Mr Rafał Weber from Stalowa Wola. The rapid and efficient construction of the Central Industrial District is a major economic and civilizational leap of the south-central part of Poland, and thus of the Second Republic of Poland. You can give an example of 300 km of the Gorlice - Jasło - Krosno - Ostrowiec pipeline. It is also worth mentioning that next to Eugeniusz Kwiatkowski, one of the people who deserved the COP idea, as well as for the construction of the port in Gdynia, was General Kazimierz Sosnkowski, about whom the historian wrote: He laid the foundations for the Polish military industry and carried out the initial phase of the construction of the later Central Industrial District. The Central Industrial District, this great achievement of independent Poland, deserves to be reminded by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, even if we hear it, because we have already heard such absurd sentences here, e.g. that the COP builders were inconclusive because they fought as legionaries of Piłsudski and then soldiers of the Polish Army with Moskals, who did not occupy Poland, but so friendlyly defended rights and freedoms. The draft resolution was discussed at the meeting of the cultural committee on 24 May this year. and, following the introduction of quite numerous but very minor amendments, received a unanimous positive opinion. I present the draft resolution. The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the celebration of the 80th anniversary of the beginning of the construction of the Central Industrial District. 80 years ago the construction of the Central Industrial District and its flagship investment - Stalowa Wola began. The result of this effort was the creation of 110 000 jobs in 51 new industrial plants, built in civilised areas that were so far backwards in 2.5 years. The initiator of the investment was Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of the Treasury Eugeniusz Kwiatkowski, who in the 20s gave Poland a window to the world in the form of a port in Gdynia, and already in the next decade, after years of the global crisis, outlined and implemented a plan to industrialize central Poland, ensuring a balance between arms investments, civil investments and infrastructure according to the motto: COP is not only military weapons, but also economic weapons of Poland. The symbol and essence of these achievements is the construction of modern Southern Works and the city of Stalowa Wola. Within 26 months and 26 days in the sandy pine forest, the area crossed by the San River, the national road and the railway line was built a quality steel factory and a cannon factory and a town settlement for 4,000 inhabitants equipped with modern, comfortable apartments, shops, schools and sports facilities. Such a pace of work, combined with perfect organization of works, high quality of execution, application of modern technologies and cost-effectiveness in implementation, was characteristic for all investments of the Central Industrial District. Those successes were the basis for their success. not only the high professionality of industrial personnel, but also its rooting in values such as honesty, responsibility, concern for common good and patriotism manifested in pride in the work done for the independent - after so long slavery - the Homeland. It is worth remembering that in the résumés of both Deputy Prime Minister Eugeniusz Kwiatkowski and many COP employees, boastful cards were fighting in the Legions for independence and in the war with the Soviets - for its defence. In the Second Republic of Poland, soldiers became darts organizers, precise engineers, inventors, dedicated teachers, solid merchants and reliable officials. On the 80th anniversary of the beginning of the construction of the Central Industrial District of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland expresses appreciation and respect for our ancestors, who in difficult and uncertain times of the 20th interwar anniversary and during the first years of independence of Poland pursued its economic development. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I recommend that the High Sejm adopt a resolution on the celebration of the 80th anniversary of the start of the construction of the Central Industrial District. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u574	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present our position on the draft resolution commemorating the 80th anniversary of the start of the construction of the Central Industrial District. The Law and Justice Club is an applicant for this project, so of course it is very positive towards it. We believe that it is our duty to commemorate the start of the construction of the Central Industrial District as a plan to build a fully independent and independent state based on Polish technical thought. COP is the largest economic and economic investment of the Second Republic of Poland and one of the most important in the 1050-year history of Poland. The construction of COP was initiated by Deputy Prime Minister for Economic Affairs and Minister of the Treasury Eugeniusz Kwiatkowski. Originally, the COP construction plan was to be implemented throughout Poland, but due to the limited measure it was decided to modify and concentrate investments in the south-central parts of the country, in the so-called. A safety triangle. The aim was to make a significant part of the investment beyond the reach of German and Soviet aviation. The Vistula and Sanu basin, protected from the south by the Carpathian chain, was considered the best place. With its scope COP covered areas of Podkarpackie, Świętokrzyskie, Małopolskie and partly Mazowieckie voivodships. In total, this area covered 60 000. km2 and it had 5600 thousand inhabitants. more than 100,000 people were created as part of the Central Industrial District plan. jobs, of which as much as 90 000. in industry. When replacing COP's investments, it is important to mention Dębica, where a rubber factory and a tyre factory were built, about Mielec and State Aviation Works, in Nowa Dębie a ammunition factory was established, in Rzeszów - a factory of aircraft machine tools and engines, in Tarnobrzeg - metalworking plants, in Nowa Sarzyn - chemical plants, in Lublin - a factory of trucks, in Radom and Starachowice - a factory of weapons, in Pionki - a gunpowder factory. The flagship investment was the Southern Plant producing weapons equipment and the city of Stalowa Wola built by them. The plants and the city were built within 26 months and 26 days, thus showing the pace of work and consistency in the construction of the Central Industrial District. Besides, on the day of their opening, General Kasprzycki said that it was the steel will of the Polish people to become modern. Hence the name of the city itself. COP's plan deserves a unique memory, because it was based on great patriotism and love for Poland. Deputy Prime Minister Kwiatkowski himself fought in the First World War in the Polish Legions, just as many of those who created production plants after 1937 and then worked in them. At this point in time, it appears that on 25 November 2015, when the National Council of Agricultural Chambers requested the Seed Agency in Leszno to waive the levy on farmers for the use of seeding material from their own collection, the agency replied that breeding companies could not waive the basic source of funding for breeding, such as licence fees and exclusive rights for the use of varieties under the agricultural derogation. The essence of the development of breeding is the cultivation of varieties with high health and performance qualities, resistant to dangerous pathogens and pests. Such varieties may be grown with limited use of pesticides. The cultivation of such varieties prevents excessive degradation of the environment and reduces production costs, which is equivalent to increasing competitiveness. In Poland the development of both breeding and agriculture inhibits the small exchange of certified seed. In this respect, we are in the last place in Europe and this is not without an impact on the quality of Polish production. At the moment, farmers must inform the seed agency of sowing and be subject to control and, in the event of failure to comply with these obligations, their cases shall be brought before the courts. In this context, it may be worth considering the Scandinavian model that was discussed in the agriculture committee when discussing the project. It is based on the fact that breeders' or breeders' organisations are informed that the farmer has used material for reproduction. The information is handled by an agency similar to the Polish Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, which collects information for the purposes of a single area payment and on this basis issues invoices. This avoids extensive bureaucracy related to requests for information, checks, judicial matters, and also reduces fees when this model is universal. This is a solution that the Ministry should take into account. Because earlier because of what I was talking about, we didn't support this project, [[♪ The bell ♪]] we are also in favour of rejecting the Senate's amendments. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u602	We will proceed to the vote on the Senate resolution in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 29 of the agenda: Report from the Health Committee on the Government's draft law on how to determine the lowest basic salary of workers engaged in medical professions employed in medicinal entities (prints 1583 and 1617). I ask Mrs Barbara Dziuk to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u603	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present to the High House a report by the Health Committee on the Government's draft law on how to determine the lowest basic salary of medical professionals employed in medicinal entities, Parliamentary Printing No 1617. The project involves introducing a minimum wage in health care into the Polish legal order. It sets out how to determine the lowest basic salary of medical professionals employed in medicinal entities, taking into account the type of work carried out, and how and schedule of the investigation to the established level of basic salary. Medical workers have been waiting for systemic minimum wage regulation for many years, unfortunately without an answer from the governments of POs and PSL. Failure to introduce the solutions that are currently under review to guarantee the lowest level of pay for medical workers has led to a reduction, as part of the easiest way to find savings, pay or freezing in recent years, sometimes drastically low wages in health care. The aim of the proposed law is not to establish the conditions for remuneration for the work of workers performing medical professions in such a way as to create the so-called. wage grids, but setting the lowest basic salaries, i.e. a minimum level below which the employer cannot set the basic salary. The establishment of the lowest basic salaries will increase the salaries of the least earning medical staff in the various professional groups. The mechanism used to increase salaries which differentiates the level of their increase depending on the level of the employee's basic salary to date will result in the least-earned workers receiving the highest increases. Such a mechanism will undoubtedly contribute to reducing wage disparities within the various occupational groups. It should be stressed that the target amounts of the lowest basic salaries laid down in the draft law were set at a level similar to the current average basic salaries of the different groups of medical professions. The draft law contained in the committee's report presented by me, unlike in the case of the universal cross-sectoral minimum wage, establishes the lowest basic salary. Such a solution is beneficial for medical workers due to their sustainability and the additional effect of the increase in the remaining components of remuneration, as well as the increase in the basic salary. Height of allowances, e.g. an internship, for medical work, night work, Sundays and holidays, as you know, depends on the amount of the basic salary. The legislation in force in previous years introduced increases in health care did not specify what elements of remuneration are to be increased. This led most directors to raise salaries in the form of temporary wage allowances, which were easy to collect, creating a feeling of uncertainty and lack of financial stability among employees. Turning to the specific regulations contained in the proposal, I would like to briefly bring up a few. According to the draft, medicinal products will be required to increase the basic salary of medical professionals by 31 December 2021. In turn, from 1 January 2022, the lowest basic salary laid down by the Act will not increase, but the basic salary of medical professionals will not be lower than that fixed at 31 December 2021. The lowest basic remuneration will be the product of the basic amount, i.e. the amount announced by the President of the CSO of the average monthly gross remuneration in the national economy in the year preceding the determination, and the labour coefficient set out in the annex to the draft law, and during the period from the date of entry into force of the Act until 31 December 2019 the basic amount was set at a frozen level of PLN 3900 gross. However, when regulating salaries in 2020 and 2021, employers will already be required to take into account the average monthly gross remuneration in the national economy of the year preceding the establishment. In the period from 1 July 2017 to 31 December 2021, the proposed law introduces a transitional period during which medicinal products will carry out gradual annual increases in basic salaries in order to achieve the lowest wage targets. The project sets minimum guaranteed basic wage increases of at least 10% in 2017 the amount of the difference between the lowest basic salary and the basic salary of the worker concerned, and thereafter between 1 July 2018 and 1 July 2021 by an amount of not less than 20% the remaining difference between the lowest basic salary fixed for the year concerned and the current basic salary of the worker concerned. The distribution of the investigation process to target minimum wages over the next four years will allow medicinal and public finances to implement the provisions of the Act without endangering the financial stability of both individual medicinal entities and the health system as a whole. The project also includes a dedicated solution for non-medical professionals. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 5 provides that, in agreement with the staff member or in the direction of the manager or the formation body, the rules for raising the remuneration of non-medical workers shall also be laid down in such a way that their remuneration corresponds in particular to the type of work carried out and the qualifications required for the performance of the work. The draft also provides for mechanisms to control the implementation of the law. The audit will include, on the one hand, medicinal entities and, on the other hand, the bodies of the State Labour Inspection as an institution specialised in controlling labour law compliance, including in particular the provisions with the lowest remunerations. I would like to inform you that during the first reading, one amendment was introduced to the project, stating that in 2017, the agreement under which salary increases will be made in specific medicinal entities will be concluded within one month of the date of entry into force of the Act. In the following years, the agreements will be concluded by 31 May each year. This amendment, which is a transitional provision, will remove doubts that might arise in the first year of application of the Law. High Seym! Mr. Marshal! The Health Committee welcomes the bill and asks for the draft to be adopted in the wording presented. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u647	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Health Commission on the draft law on how to determine the lowest basic salary of medical professionals employed in medicinal entities. The project will certainly systematically regulate the rules for determining the lowest basic salary of medical professionals employed in medicinal entities. I am also convinced that it will help to eliminate the sense of social injustice currently prevailing among those workers who are remunerated according to different principles. The proposed law will introduce provisions to ensure the protection of the basic salary, and those who earn the least will be guaranteed a gradual increase in their salary. It is also important that the law sets only the lower limit of remuneration and not their upper rate. According to the draft law, the first salary increase is foreseen for 1 July 2017 and further stages will be introduced gradually in order not to disrupt the financial structure of medicinal entities. In its intention, the proposed law is to encourage interest in training in medical professions and later employment in our country. And in this respect, I have the most doubts. The proposed rates, of course, increase today's salary. However, it will not be attractive enough to compete with offers from this professional area in other European countries or offers from other professions. In my view, therefore, it is important to seek to increase wages to the level of attractiveness in relation to existing labour alternatives. This is particularly important, as was also raised during the work in committee, because among others. Polish nurses and midwives are currently a professional group, most of which are workers closer to the retirement age. Given the rate of aging of the Polish population and the need for medical care, this is a very worrying indicator. The bill assigns medical staff employed in medical entities to 10 professional groups. However, it does not take into account the interests of certain groups of workers, e.g. hospital pharmacists, as suggested by their representative during the committee's deliberations. I am also anxious to observe the omission in the law of the entire environment of addictive therapists, both addict psychotherapy specialists and addicting instructors. Failure to include these professions in the list of medical staff will make it possible for the directors of the ZOZs to freely interpret the provisions of the Act. It discredits their skills and can cause financial degradation. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u895	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, My statement today is devoted to the man for whom the Sejm of the Republic of Poland established 2018 as the Year of Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk. When he was born on February 1, 1918, Simon and Maria Tokarczuk were a family of peasants. He was born as the fourth son in the family, but the three previous sons died as a child. So the parents made vows: if Ignacy survives, they will give thanks to the statue of Our Lady. They staged it in 1928 when the boy turned 10. The event took place in the Upper Lubiankas near Szbarzyń on the Ends of the Second Republic. Ignacy Tokarczuk was a peer of the Second Republic. He grew up with her as a middle-class host. Early came the recognition of the calling to serve the Lord God. In 1937, he joined a seminary in Lviv and became a student of the Theological Faculty of the University of Jan Kazimierz. He received priestly ordination in the Lviv Cathedral on 21 June 1942 at the hands of Fr. bp. Eugene Baziak. Like most Poles on the South-East Fr. Ignacy Tokarczuk experienced many totalitarianisms of the 20th century. He survived, even though he had to hide from the Red Army. He miraculously avoided arresting the Germans, and UPA issued a death sentence on him. His loved ones died at the hands of the Ukrainians. In 1945, when under the agreements in Yalta we lost Polish Kresa, he was forced to leave Lviv and the parish of St. Mary Magdalene. He never again visited the country of childhood years, but the fate of the Church and Poles was always his concern and emotion. He followed the repatriates to the Western lands, where the Poles were settled, expelled from their fatherhood. He worked among them as a vicar of the parish of St. Christ the King in Katowice. Here the party activists did not like its dynamism and ambiguity. That's always been the case. Later life Fr. Ignacy Tokarczuk was made up of many miracles. One of them was the time when the iron prime minister Joseph Cyrankiewicz did not see his nomination and on 6 February 1966 in the Przemysław Cathedral a strong opponent of the commune, a cardinal. Stefan Wyszyński received the bishop's sacre from the Primate of Poland. In his service he became famous for his uncompromising attitude towards the Polish People's Republic. By the communist state's apparatus, he was regarded as a strong opponent of the socialist system by the structure of the emerging "Solidarity" as a husband of providence and a great advocate of civil liberties. In the homilies he called things by name, and we laymen prepared for life in a sovereign country. He built a spirit of struggle for his own, supporting peasant strikes in Usrzych and Rzeszów. He had an opinion on the talks with the Communists in 1988 by activists of the "Solidarity" centered around Lech Wałęsa. He thought it was a waste of time. Hence so many examples of his criticism of the so-called. A thick line. When he first took the initiative to return catechesis to schools after the 1989 changes, he met with opposition from the government. He never bowed, even though his life was wired. When he didn't get permission to build a church in Jasl, he said, "I won't ask again." Thus, without the permission of the authorities of the Polish People's Republic, he built about 400 churches and chapels and created 220 new parishes, and the communist authorities did not keep up with administrative processes. For all this he was recognized by the President of the Republic of Poland Prof. Lech Kaczyński in 2006 and awarded the highest Polish award - the Order of the White Eagle. Today, on behalf of the agricultural environment, I express my great thanks and appreciation for the initiative to establish 2018 the Year of Archbishop Ignacy Tokarczuk. And before his beautiful attitude and personality, I bow my forehead in gratitude for the good he has received. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-08-sejm-43-2.u901	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! For several years already annually, in June, at the Cemetery - mausoleum of Martyrology in Olsztyn k. Częstochowa celebrates a festive Mass dedicated to heroes fighting for the freedom of their homeland murdered in 1939 and 1945. In 1940, the Gestapo established a place of destruction in the forest on the road from Olsztyn to Kusiąt. This area was previously known by the local population as the "Shubienice," probably hanged here by convicts. Already at the time of the partitions by order of the Tsarian authorities. The first German execution took place here in June 1940. 14 people were shot down then. Both these and other executions from that period involved inter alia. with plans of extermination of Polish intelligence implemented by the Germans. Among the victims were prisoners from Radomska and Częstochowa. They included representatives of local elites, as well as people suspected of conspiracy. They were all victims of German AB action, which was arrested by officials, politicians, Catholic priests, teachers, professionals and even craftsmen, students and students. The execution was carried out by the Gestapo. Hitlerov action AB, also known as an extraordinary pacification action, was conducted from May to September 1940. According to the Nazi stereotype of the Pole, only intelligence has a national consciousness, and by destroying it, it will be possible to transform the rest of the Polish society into a mass serving as an unskilled labour force. The genocide of Polish elites in the first years of World War II was a joint venture between the German authorities and the Soviet Union. Since the beginning of the occupation, Soviet and Nazi terror has been directed primarily at Polish intelligence and higher layers. The two most famous extermination actions against the Polish elite are German AB and Soviet Katyn crime, both carried out in 1940. They were preceded by joint conferences, during which German Gestapo and Soviet NKVD officers exchanged information and experience. Under Olsztyn, the Germans also murdered members of the resistance movement and local residents. After 1941, the Nazis also shot soldiers of the Red Army from POW camps located in Częstochowa and Olsztyn. Those prisoners were then particularly cruelly treated by the Germans. In common graves in the forest cemetery of the mausoleum of Martyrology there are 1968 victims of Nazism, most of the inhabitants of nearby Częstochowa, representatives of Polish intelligence, including teachers from Częstochowa schools. Later, many political prisoners and resistance activists from Częstochowa died here. In the '60s. This place was duly commemorated in the 20th century by establishing a mausoleum cemetery, which 20 years later was decorated with a relief depicting the scene of the shooting by Władysław Łyczba. On the road there was also a monument to the direction indicator. A few years ago, the cemetery was renovated, renovated and crossroaded. The inscription at the foot of the obelisk is: The nation will never forget them. The annual festivities, the celebrated Mass in the place of doom, are a tribute which we today live to the heroes of those days. So important is the message: Anything that has been a souvenir for us today can be written tomorrow with the dust of oblivion. The abyss in eternity without leaving traces for future generations. It is the duty of the living to gather meadows and crumbs of the most expensive souvenirs of the past. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]
